Also: **the `artifact` multipart part must be a JSON `Blob`, not a string** — `@RequestPart` picks its converter from the part's own `Content-Type` and a string part arrives `text/plain`, failing as a `415` about media types nowhere near the field it is about; `artifact.attachment_id` and `owner_type='ARTIFACT'` are **both written and are not rivals**, so the reader who assumes one relationship was declared twice deletes the wrong half; `Artifact.register` takes the id as a **parameter** because setting the column on a *managed* instance compiles, runs and persists nothing; and `Artifact`'s "four deliberately unmapped columns" is three now — **the fifth blocker note in this repo to outlive its blocker**. **SEVENTH DRIFT OF THE FRONTEND TEST COUNT, measured on both sides**: phase 3's commit claims 1045, a clean run at `e95ef1a` measures **1055/79**, so phase 4's real delta is **+33/+4** **(built and unverified — 11 browser rows `M2-62`…`M2-66`, `M3-65`…`M3-69`, `F-8`…`F-10`, which author their own fixtures including a zero-byte and a 25MB file; `M3-38` CORRECTED IN PLACE because it asserted the absence of the control this slice adds; three absences stated rather than left to look like oversights)** |
| [§127](handoff/127-the-four-unrendered-block-types.md) | The four unrendered block types — decision round 3's step 7 (#48), three phases, no migration: `DocBlockType` has had six values since M0 and **two were rendered**, so a documentation space could store a table and render it as a caption and store an image and render **nothing**. Two of the four had no *data* to render — `BlockContent` grew `rows` and `attachmentId`, needing no migration because the column is JSONB and the record's day-one `ignoreUnknown` was written against exactly this. **THE FINDING IS `PageVersions`**: `recordEdit` writes a version only when the snapshot's hash moved, and the snapshot is built field by field — so a content field it does not carry is one somebody can rewrite **for ever** while the history says nothing happened, with the page saving and reading back correct. Every other table assertion passes against that version. `@JsonInclude(NON_NULL)` on the two **new** components and deliberately not the older two, because otherwise every stored `content_hash` in every deployment changes on deploy and the first save of each untouched page writes an empty-diff version — and **nothing inside the suite can see that**, since every hash in a run is computed by one build, so the assertion is that a paragraph's *stored* snapshot has no `rows` key. Three payload fields, one rule (**refused** on the types that lack them). **The first table row is the header and there is no flag** — a boolean's two states look identical in an editor and differ only in what a screen reader announces for every cell below. **Ragged is refused, not padded**, and row 0 decides the width, because an author's empty cell and a server's invented one are indistinguishable and mean opposite things. An image's `text` is **alt text and blank is refused**, one `else if` from the empty-paragraph rule that must stay legal; an image block **cannot be created with its page** and the refusal says the sequence rather than reporting a valid id as unknown. The attachment delete is a **409 with no foreign key behind it** — the reference is inside a JSONB document — run **before the bytes**, which do not come back. Phase 2: **there were TWO `BlockView`s branching on `blockType` and they DISAGREED**, so one stored outline read as an outline on one screen and flat prose on the other, with nothing failing; `DocBlockBody` is the one renderer, the `default` arm is **not dead code** (the server may be a version ahead of the bundle), and **`<img src>` answers 401 silently** — `useAttachmentBlob` caches the **`Blob` and never an object URL**, because a URL is a lifetime and a revoked one in a shared cache breaks the next mount. That is **not** a reversal of §126's no-thumbnail decision: there the cost was every file of a page fetched to decorate a list, here the picture *is* the content. Phase 3: the editor could author three of six types, and **`level` could be read and never written** since §93 — the writerless shape one field down. A **grid, not a delimited textarea**, so ragged is unconstructable rather than merely refused **(built and unverified — 9 browser rows `M3-70`…`M3-78` plus `F-11`, and 14 mutation rows `DB-1`…`DB-14`, none run; ONE FULL FRONTEND RUN IN FOUR WENT RED WITH 2 FAILURES, unreproduced across three later runs and its test names NOT CAPTURED)** |
| [§128](handoff/128-the-draft-buffer.md) | The draft buffer, and the check `If-Match` structurally cannot make — decision round 3's step 8 (#57), two phases, no migration: `test_case_draft` has existed since `V1` with a comment describing this exact flow and **no code behind it**, so the case editor had no autosave at all and closing a tab lost the work. §6j was settled as one sentence — *"versions written on explicit save only; autosave goes to a draft buffer"* — and **only the first clause shipped**, in §29. **THERE IS NO PROMOTE ENDPOINT AND THERE MUST NOT BE**: `V1`'s comment says a save "promotes this into `test_case`", which reads like an operation the service should offer, and it would be a **second write path into `test_case` whose input is by construction unvalidated** — either duplicating `CaseEdit` (two definitions of a legal case, free to disagree) or skipping it. Promotion is the client restoring into the form and the user pressing Save. **THE FINDING IS `lockVersion`, and it is a lost update the existing machinery is structurally blind to**: Ana drafts at v3, Bob saves making it v4, Ana returns, the editor loads the case **fresh** at v4 and offers her draft, she accepts and saves — with `If-Match: 4`, which is *correct*, so the stale-editor check passes and Bob's change is overwritten by text written before it existed. The check compares what the client **read** against what is stored, and the client did read the current version; nothing asks what the **draft** was written against. `stale` is computed server-side and is **advisory** — the words are the author's, and dropping them would be the product deciding somebody else's edit invalidates your sentence. **THE BUFFER IS PER CASE, NOT PER PERSON** — `case_id` *is* the primary key — so two people editing one case share it and a colleague's save clears your draft; `updatedBy` is therefore **on the wire** rather than an internal audit column, because restoring silently would publish somebody's half-finished sentence under your name. Both consequences are pinned by tests that state what the shape **is**, so a later per-person key changes a test rather than changing behaviour quietly. Nothing in `content` is validated (that is what a buffer means), so **a draft that restores cleanly can still be refused on Save**; no `@Version`, because an autosave answering 409 is one the user cannot resolve; `case:edit` on all three verbs **including the read**, because a draft is somebody's unfinished sentence and a Viewer is invited to read coverage rather than watch it being written; 256 KB in **bytes**, because nothing else bounds a row written by a timer into a table no retention prunes. **204 is not the 404 beside it** — almost every case has no draft almost always, and collapsing them tells an editor their case is gone on the ordinary path (§35's argument at a different status code). **THE RED RUN WAS THE FIXTURE AND THE COLLISION WAS NOT A UUID**: all 26 tests died in `@BeforeEach` on `cd-acme`, already `CaseDeleteEndpointTest`'s company **slug** and email domain — both globally unique, neither a UUID. §19's family, **sixth instance**, through the fields the convention does not name. Then three survivors failed on a minimal `PATCH` body, because a save must carry the whole editable set — the same asymmetry one level down: **the draft may be a fragment and the save may not**. **Phase 2 is the editor, and THE ORDERING IS THE DESIGN**: autosave must not run until the restore prompt is *answered*, or the first keystroke overwrites the draft the editor is a moment from offering — so the user is shown a restore prompt for work that no longer exists and, accepting it, gets back what they had already saved, with nothing erroring and nothing logged. The gate lives in the editor rather than the hook, because a hook that guessed would be a second opinion about a sequence only the screen can see. A **failed** draft read opens the gate too, deliberately: the alternative is an autosave that silently never starts for the rest of the session. `toCasePayload` moved into `caseSchema` because autosave is its **second** consumer — a second hand-written copy is how a field wired into Save and forgotten in the buffer **restores as empty**, indistinguishable from never having been typed, with both requests individually valid (`BlockContent.of`'s rule, §127 phase 1, one slice earlier). `fromCasePayload` is the inverse and **decodes defensively**, because the buffer is unvalidated by design and may have been written by an older build. **THE FIX THAT LOOKED LIKE THE BUG: `reset(values, { keepDirty: true })`** preserves the dirty flag *as it stands*, and on mount it stands at **false** — so a restored draft landed in a form the editor considered pristine, Save disabled with *"Nothing has changed yet"* over a form full of unsaved work and the autosave gated off with it; `keepDefaultValues` re-evaluates against the saved case, which is the question actually being asked. A failed autosave is a status line and **no toast**, because the caller is a timer. **THE CREATE FORM HAS NO AUTOSAVE, asserted rather than assumed** — the key is a foreign key to `test_case`, so there is nothing to key a draft on until the case exists **(built and unverified — 9 browser rows `M2-67`…`M2-75` plus `F-12`, of which `M2-67` cannot be reached by any gate because the point is surviving something the suite cannot do, closing the tab, and `M2-71` needs two accounts and two browsers; 16 mutation rows `CD-1`…`CD-16`, NONE run)** |
| [§129](handoff/129-clone-case-plan-run.md) | Clone case, plan and run — decision round 3's step 8 second half (#59), two phases, no migration: **a clone is a create**, so the service reads the source, builds the request the create endpoint already takes and delegates, and **six of §6v's clone rules hold by construction** rather than by being remembered (new display key, DRAFT status, own version 1, no shared history, no execution history, idempotency). **THE RED RUN IS THE FINDING**: §6v states *"copy the automation status"* and *"`automationKey` is never copied"* independently and they are **jointly unsatisfiable** — `CaseEdit.validate` refuses `AUTOMATED` with no key, so a clone of an automated case was simply impossible and neither document said so. `AUTOMATED` becomes `TO_BE_AUTOMATED`, which is not a compromise but the accurate state (intended to be automated, no automation yet — exactly what §6c makes the key optional for); relaxing the validator instead leaves a case reporting as automated that §117's matcher can never match, for ever. **§6v's "attachments by reference, not byte copy" is the one rule in that table that CANNOT work here**: `AttachmentStore` holds no reference count and cannot acquire one without learning what a row is (§125's property), so two rows over one `storage_key` means deleting **either** destroys the other's bytes and the survivor renders as a file that 404s with nothing failing. `copy` is a **fifth** method on that port rather than `open()`-then-`put()` in the caller — `put` takes a `byte[]`, so the caller version forces every byte of every file through heap for ever and forecloses the one operation an S3 adapter makes free — and **`ReportStore` deliberately does not get it**, the first divergence of the two types §125 kept apart so a wrong one would not compile. `Attachment.copyForCase` **carries** the source's checksum rather than recomputing it: recomputing hashes the bytes *as they are now*, so a silently rotted source would hand its clone a fresh attestation that the rot is correct. **Phase 2 is where the design breaks**: "a clone is a create" holds for the case and the run and **does not hold for the plan**, because `PlanWriteRequest` has six fields where `PlanUpdateRequest` has twelve — deliberately, *a plan is named before anybody has subdivided it* — so a delegated clone drops the tag arm, every §97 section, both prose fields and both criteria lists and answers **201**. Create-then-edit is worse than it looks (v1 describes a plan with no sections and no criteria, v2 lands a second later, so every clone is born with a history saying somebody rewrote it), so `PlanCreateService.write` was extracted to take a validated `PlanEdit` instead of a request, and `TestPlan.create` stopped hard-coding `entryCriteria = null` — the null still comes from `validateCreate`, which is where that policy belongs. **THAT EXTRACTION SURFACED A THIRD FINDING: the scope→rows mapping existed TWICE** — five arms in `PlanUpdateService`, two in `PlanCreateService`, correct only while the create request could not express the other three — so §127's `BlockContent.of` defect one aggregate over, now `PlanScopeItem.rowsOf`. Three more things the schema decided against the plan document: **a run has no assignee to copy** (the column is on `test_execution`, one per case, so choosing one would invent a fact), **a run stores no scope at all** (§99's arms are resolved once into executions and the declaration discarded, so a cloned suite-scoped run does **not** pick up the cases added since — §6v's own clone-versus-template distinction, for once decided by the schema), and **§6g's approval gate is re-evaluated with the override never inherited**, which no line mentions because it follows from the clone being a create. Also: the name is **required** on all three and the server invents no `Copy of …`; provenance is a **second** audit row (`*.cloned`) naming both display keys; a plan's criteria are copied as stored JSON and **not re-validated**, or a retired metric would make a plan permanently uncloneable; the plan clone's `plan:create`-alone gate is pinned by a check **derived from the seeded catalogue**; condition opt-outs are not copied because `CaseConditionOptout` has no writer anywhere in `src/main`; and §6v rule 4's deep-vs-shallow suite question is **superseded by §98** **(built and unverified — 30 mutation rows `CL-1`…`CL-30`, none run; NO browser rows in either phase, deliberately. THE FRONTEND IS PHASE 3 AND IS NOT BUILT: all three endpoints have no consumer in `src/`, the shape §124's `artifact:delete` shipped in)** |
| [§130](handoff/130-the-autosave-subscription.md) | The autosave subscription that re-rendered the whole case editor — **not a slice, a CI red**: §128's `const values = watch()` in `CaseEditorPage` re-rendered the entire 1,400-line editor on every keystroke (21 page renders per 20 characters; 4,574 ms to type twenty characters into Title), and it surfaced as a 30 s **timeout** rather than as a complaint about typing. `CaseDraftStatus` owns the subscription and the status line now, so the per-keystroke render is a `Box`; `useWatch` subscribes and `getValues` reads, split so the payload stays typed as `CaseInput` rather than a deep-partial behind a cast — the drift `toCasePayload` exists to prevent. Two dead ends recorded: `delay: null` changes nothing (the cost is render work, not the wait) and a `watch()`→`getValues()` swap measured nothing (it broke three autosave tests whose timeouts swamped it) — **a render counter is what settled it**. `vitest.config.ts` records this budget being raised 5 s → 20 s → 30 s and says each raise treats the symptom; this is the cause one of the five files was circling, **and the other four still cross 30 s under load while importing none of it** |
| [§131](handoff/131-the-outbox.md) | The outbox — decision round 3's **step 9, the round's last**: `V1` shipped the table in M0 with a comment and no code, and `CompanyScopedEvent` has asserted since M0 that a failed handler lands there, with **three later files quoting the absence as a premise for their own design**. **THE FINDING IS THAT THE OUTBOX IS NOT ONLY DURABILITY — it is the only way two feature modules may exchange a shape**: plan §3's rule has no exemption for an event *type* (`ArchitectureRulesTest` reads every dependency out of a module), so `reporting` cannot import an event class from `execution` and the obvious Spring shape — a typed event and a `@TransactionalEventListener` — is **unbuildable between two feature modules in this codebase**. `event_type TEXT` + `payload JSONB` is the mechanism, not a storage detail. **`V1`'s partial index decided the status semantics**: `outbox_pending_idx` is `WHERE status = 'PENDING'`, so a retrying row must stay `PENDING` with the schedule pushed out or it falls off the only index the schema has for it — which leaves `FAILED` (ceiling reached on something that might stop happening; replayable) and `DEAD` (structurally undeliverable) meaning different things, and **an unclaimed event type is `DEAD` and never `SENT`**, because a delivered-looking row nobody consumed is §47/§53/§72/§81 again. **THREE TRANSACTIONS PER EVENT AND THE HANDLER IS IN NONE OF THEM**: one transaction around everything is poisoned by the handler's own failure (Spring marks it rollback-only, so the attempt count and error message are discarded and the row retries for ever having recorded nothing), and a lock held across the handler holds a pooled connection across somebody else's network call — the exact thing `V1`'s comment is about. What replaces the lock is a **lease**: `claim` bumps `attempts` and pushes `next_attempt_at`, status stays `PENDING`, so a crash costs one interval rather than a stuck row, and `attempts` moving at the *claim* is what makes a payload that kills the process exhaust rather than loop. The price is at-least-once, carried as a written contract. Two refusals for defects with no symptom: recording outside the producing transaction (everything still passes and the guarantee is gone) and dispatching inside one. **The build changed the design twice.** Several handlers may claim one type — the first draft failed startup on duplicates, which was right about code and wrong about the product, since the event moves both `reporting`'s fact log and `repository`'s health signals and neither may call the other. And **`recordCoalesced` takes no payload and no aggregate**: fifty verdicts in a run would be fifty whole-company recomputes, and a coalesced event stands for every change since the last delivery, so naming one of them is a fact about one presented as the reason for all — it folds only into a row with `attempts = 0`, because a claimed row may already have read the database before this write committed. Phase 2's producer and both consumers were **pre-authorized verbatim** by the code they change (*"Landing `ResultRecorded` later adds a handler that calls the same service and changes nothing here"*), the schedulers stay as the rebuild path, and the event buys latency only. **The invitation email is deliberately NOT the first producer and cannot be one**: only a digest of the token is stored, so the payload would be a live credential at rest and a retry would have to mint a different token (§122's grant-vs-artifact distinction). Also: `OutboxEvents` puts the names in a foundation module for one spelling on both sides of a boundary the compiler cannot see; **no `SKIP LOCKED`**, which the plan called for, because the claim's lock spans one `UPDATE` and the lease does the job skipping would; four javadocs corrected for asserting an absence this slice ends (§100's rule, fifth sighting). **THE SECOND FINDING IS A DEFECT THE WHOLE SUITE WAS STRUCTURALLY UNABLE TO SEE**: the two handlers were first written as `reporting.ResultsChangedHandler` and `repository.ResultsChangedHandler` — one simple name, two packages, both `@Component` — which is the same **default bean name**, so the context fails to start with `ConflictingBeanDefinitionException` and takes every `@SpringBootTest` with it. **And no test here would ever have reproduced it**: both carry `@ConditionalOnProperty` and `IntegrationTestBase` turns *both* properties off for reasons that predate this slice, conditions are evaluated during scanning, so neither bean was ever registered and the two names never met. It would have fired exactly once — on the first boot of a real deployment. The rename is not the fix; `ArchitectureRulesTest.springStereotypesHaveDistinctSimpleNames` is, and it reads **bytecode rather than a running context**, because a context-based check inherits the very blindness that hid it. **NO RED RUN in either phase** **(built and unverified — 21 mutation rows `OB-1`…`OB-21`, none run; no browser rows, deliberately. NO operator screen for `FAILED`/`DEAD` rows — M6 — recorded as a known absence)** |
| [§132](handoff/132-token-attribution.md) | Token attribution — **the first slice after decision round 3 closed, and re-derived from the code rather than from a list** (§28/§29/§46); also the MCP WIP plan's §7 phase 1, which its own note calls independent of everything below it. Three phases, **no migration and no contract change**: `V1` gave the schema three places to record that a *credential* acted and all three were empty — `audit_event.actor_token_id` mapped and mentioned by no Java outside the entity, `test_execution.executed_by_token` **not mapped at all** while four files discussed it in prose, and `api_token.last_used_at` mapped with a getter and no setter. §116 issued the first token and §117 made CI ingest with one, so all three have had a writer's worth of traffic since M3 and recorded none of it: a CI job's audit row said a *person* did it, the history read `ingested by CI` for every credential on the instance, and *"revoke the ones nobody has used"* could not be answered. **Each carried a javadoc asserting its own emptiness and two were already false** (§100's rule, sixth and seventh sighting). **THE FINDING IS THAT `TokenContext` HAD WRITTEN DOWN THE CONDITION UNDER WHICH IT WOULD HAVE TO MOVE, AND THIS SLICE MET IT.** `AuditEvent.record` has 42 call sites and none gained a parameter, because that type's own header refuses the shape for the permission bound — and the argument is *sharper* for attribution: a forgotten bound defaults to the unbounded answer, a forgotten attribution defaults to *"a person did this"*, a false record that reads correct. So `record` reads the ambient token as it already reads the ambient company, which required moving `TokenContext` from `identity` to `tenancy` (`AuditEvent` lives there because every module writes to it, and `tenancy` may not import `identity`). **The move is what made a missing guard buildable**: `TokenContext.clear()` had recorded an exposure — a pooled worker inheriting a leaked token — and argued it away with *"this context only ever narrows"*, while naming its own expiry, *"it stops holding the moment anything reads this context to grant rather than to bound"*. Reading it to **attribute** is neither and breaks the asymmetry from the other side: an inherited token makes a scheduled job's audit row name a credential that had nothing to do with it. `CompanyContextTaskDecorator` clears both contexts now. **Both actor columns are written, never one instead of the other.** Phase 2 mapped `executed_by_token` and resolved it to the token's **name** on the way out (`ExecutionTokens`, a derived `findAllByIdIn` because `EntityManager.find` skips the `@Filter`; the name is unique only among *live* tokens, so it is ambiguous across a rotation and that is recorded rather than designed away) — and it is **not exclusive with `executedBy`**, since Automation Service holds `result:submit` and a credential posting one verdict fills both. **The red run was `Map.of().get(null)` — §38's trap, SEVENTH sighting, one slice after §121 phase 4 recorded the sixth in the words this code then copied without the behaviour**: `tokenNames` is `Map.of()` whenever no row on the page named a token, which is the *ordinary* case, so every case history 500'd and **eighteen tests went red, none of them about tokens**. **The second finding is in the SPA**: the author caption branched on the run's `source` — a property of the run — while the actor is a property of the verdict, so a credential posting into a MANUAL run rendered as *"by \<the owner's name\>"*, a person's name on a machine's claim; no backend test can see it, and it is browser row `A3-16`. Phase 3 put the `last_used_at` bump in the auth service's **second** transaction (the one already open inside the company — a writer in the filter gets its own, which begins outside the scope and updates zero rows under RLS: §116's red run one layer up), kept it **coarse** behind `tcms.api-token.last-used-resolution` (`PT1H`) because the naive form is one `UPDATE` per request on a busy CI token's single row, recorded **only a usable token** so "last used" is not "last presented", added **no `@Version`** for the reason the class already gives about `revoke`, and gave the token screen the **Last used** column its own header had promised would be a one-line change. **(built and unverified — 15 mutation rows `TA-1`…`TA-15` and three browser rows `A3-15`/`A3-16`/`A3-17`, none run)** |
| [§133](handoff/133-the-agent-role.md) | The `Agent` role — the MCP WIP plan's §7 **phases 2 and 3**, both `[SETTLED]`, and not blocked by that plan's §8, whose only genuinely open question is the *tool and command surface* (§6) gating phases 4 onward. Three phases, **one migration and one contract change**. `V40` seeds **`Agent`** — PROJECT scope, carried by API tokens, never held by a person — as the capability ceiling for agent access: **Test Engineer minus four**, `library:manage` (roles spec §A3 gates it by an *impact modal* an API caller never sees), `case:delete` and `evidence:delete` (both `OWN`, and `OWN` follows `api_token.owner_id`, so the agent would delete what the **human** authored) and `company:read` (a token is not a member, so it has no `V15` baseline grant). **`PermissionEvaluator` is unchanged** — the intersection that already caps a credential is the only narrowing point, so a Viewer's agent token resolves to Viewer's set with no branching written anywhere, which is the reason the ceiling is a *role*: it appears in the permission matrix and `SeedDataTest` can derive it, where a ceiling in validation code appears in neither. **THE FINDING IS THAT `assignable` IS ONE OF THREE QUESTIONS THAT HAD ALWAYS HAD THE SAME ANSWER.** Every exclusion of `Company Member` was a hardcoded UUID comparison in **five** places, which reads as five copies of one rule wanting a column — and it is not: `Company Member` is non-assignable **and** non-issuable **and** never listed, while `Agent` is non-assignable **but issuable and must be listed**, because the token-issue form is the only place it is ever chosen. So the flag drives the two `RoleGrantService` enforcement points and **nothing else**; the three that stay baseline-specific each look like a leftover and each fails differently — `WHERE assignable` on the `GET /roles` filter hides `Agent` from the token form, and the flag in `ApiTokenService.validate` makes an `Agent` token **unissuable, the one thing the role exists for** — with nothing else changing colour in 2217 tests. `V40` §2 and three comments refuse the cleanup in advance. **The second finding is that `RoleReference`'s own javadoc had already made the argument, about the other axis**: it filters by *scope* and says that offering a reference card *"next to a Grant button that will refuse it is the mis-teaching"* — a sentence about assignability written about scope, because until now every listed role was grantable somewhere. `Agent` is PROJECT-scope and grantable nowhere, the first role where the two disagree. **Three of the four SPA consumers of `GET /roles` filter on the flag and the fourth must not.** Phase 3 makes **`expiresAt` required for an `Agent` token and optional for every other role** — the first per-role rule in the issue path, in the service and not the form (the bearer works with `curl`), because the CI failure open-ended expiry avoids is not the one that happens to a credential sitting on a laptop: nobody revokes it because nobody remembers it, and an optional field is a field nobody fills in. The form pre-fills 90 days, in **local wall clock** and **only into a blank field**. **No red run in any phase**, which is stated because it is unusual; the one failure was a test of my own, where `optionNames` leaves the MUI menu open and makes the document inert. **Not built: `Automation Service` is still grantable to a person** although `V3` has called it *"attached to API tokens, never people"* since M0 — §100's rule, **eighth sighting** — and flipping it is a behaviour change with a live-data question, so it is its own slice. **(built and unverified — 9 mutation rows `AG-1`…`AG-9` and 7 browser rows `A3-18`…`A3-24`, none run; `AG-9` is a **missing test rather than a mutation** and the highest-value row: nothing drives the intersection end-to-end for an `Agent` token)** |
| [§134](handoff/134-automation-service-not-assignable.md) | Automation Service stops being grantable to a person — the slice §133 named on its way out, **two phases, one migration (`V41`) and a description-only contract change**. `V3` has called the role *"non-human, attached to API tokens, never people"* since M0 and nothing enforced it: every grant picker offered it, `POST /role-assignments` accepted it, the evaluator honoured it — **§100's rule, eighth sighting**. `V41` sets `assignable = false`; the grant, the issuability and the listing are untouched, so a CI credential is issued exactly as before and the MCP plan §2.4 decision that the role stays is unaffected. **THE FINDING IS THAT `V40`'s FLAG WAS CARRYING A FOURTH QUESTION.** `V40` §2 says it drives *"the two `RoleGrantService` enforcement points"*, grant **and** revoke, which reads as one rule and is two: refusing a grant says *this is not a role a person holds*; refusing a revoke says *removing this does damage the endpoint cannot repair*, and **exactly one row has the second** — taking the `V15` baseline away leaves a colleague seeing an empty company. For the two token ceilings the answer is the opposite, so revoke is **baseline-specific** now, the fourth member of that family. Left fused, this migration **strands data**: `AdminDirectoryService` filters only `Company Member` out of `GET /role-assignments`, so every pre-`V41` grant would sit on the screen behind a Revoke button that 403s forever with no message naming a role — §31's disable-don't-hide defect reached from the server side, with nothing failing. One slice after the flag shipped it had to be narrowed, which is not an argument against it (the grant path generalised at a cost of one line) but about when a boolean earns its name: `V40` used "assignable" to mean "assignable **or** revocable", and that reading survived exactly as long as there was one row to test it against. **Existing grants are kept, listed and revocable** — the migration deletes nothing, because a `DELETE` here removes access on a live instance with no actor and therefore no audit row it could honestly write (user decision; the alternative is recorded as refused in `AS-5`). **The second finding is that roles spec §A2's careful sentence was guarding the wrong invariant**: *"these six are the complete set of **assignable** roles"* — kept literally true by `V15` and again by `V40` — is now false, because Automation Service **is** one of the six. It is retired rather than reworded; what mattered was never *"the six are assignable"* but *"no role outside the catalogue is"*, and that is untouched. `SeedDataTest` asserts both directions, and the new one pins that **Instance Admin stays assignable** — it is refused here for a scope-shaped reason, and sweeping it in with the ceilings would put "Choose a role." on a refusal whose real message names where SYSTEM grants live. **NO FRONTEND CODE CHANGED AT ALL**, which is §133's mechanism paying for itself: three screens filter on `assignable` already and the token form carries the comment refusing that filter, so the role left three lists and stayed on the fourth on one seeded boolean. The only frontend edit is a **hand-written test fixture that had gone stale in a way no gate could catch** (`assignable: true` for the role), and its assertion is now the stronger one — the token form offers **both** non-assignable roles, where unifying the four filters makes **CI credentials unobtainable**, a louder failure than `Agent`'s. **No red run in either phase**, the second such slice in a row. **(built and unverified — 5 mutation rows `AS-1`…`AS-5` and 4 browser rows `A3-25`…`A3-28`, none run; `AS-2` first, since its failure strands data rather than merely refusing something, and `A3-27` needs a grant the UI can no longer create and says so)** |
| [§135](handoff/135-the-tool-and-command-surface.md) | The MCP tool and CLI command surface — the WIP plan's §8 question 1, its *"only genuinely blocking one"*, and the **first slice since §110 that ships no code**: a design, `docs/specs/2026-08-08-mcp-tool-and-command-surface.md`. **THE BLOCKER WAS THE ANSWER.** The plan filed "re-read the `documentation` module first" as a prerequisite to answering; it was the content of the answer. There is no `acceptance_criterion` table — but the finding is not that `list_criteria` is blocked, it is that **this product decided what a requirement is and it was never a criterion row**: ERD resolved question #18 says there is no `requirement` table, a requirement **is** a `doc_block`, and traceability is `doc_link` over blocks. So `list_criteria` is **dropped rather than deferred** — a tool for a model this product refused — and the surface §6w wanted from it already existed under another name, better, because a block id is *permanent*. `doc_link` has had a writer since §93 and its endpoint is gated `doc:edit`, **inside the `Agent` grant**, so §6w's Wave-2 `link_case_to_criterion` is a rename, not a build. **Five §6w reversals recorded with reasons**, the last of which is the §6w-era pattern again: *"every tool call writes an `audit_event` with `via = MCP`"* was written against a table that **has no `via` column**. **The surface is 21 tools over 88 paths** (plus ~45 CLI commands, two shapes over one client and one command core), and the count is an outcome of one rule — **a tool never spans two permissions on its write path**, because `tools/list` is permission-filtered, so a merged tool is either hidden from someone who could use half of it or listed for someone who 403s on half. **APPLYING THAT RULE PROPERLY CAUGHT A MERGE IN THIS DOCUMENT'S OWN FIRST DRAFT**: stated loosely it asks *"does a role hold both"* and by that test merging comments with review verdicts is safe, but **the catalogue is not what a token holds** — the effective set is an intersection, so a **Viewer**-owned agent token holds `case:comment` with no `*:review` and the merged tool would have been hidden from a caller who can legitimately comment. The question is *"does every **reachable intersection** hold both"*. **Two further merges clear only because of §134**: `Automation Service` is the one role holding `result:submit` without `run:execute` and `case:read` without `report:view`, and `V41` made it non-assignable so it is never a token *owner* — §134 turns out to be load-bearing here in three places, which was not visible when it shipped. **`POST …/cases/bulk` is excluded and it is the exclusion worth reading**: the endpoint an agent gets the most leverage from, refused because **its gate is decided by the operation inside its payload** — the same objection `documentation/package-info.java` records about payload-decided gates, one client over. *What the SPA cannot render disabled, `tools/list` cannot filter.* Eighteen tools are buildable today; two are gated on §6.1's `Idempotency-Key` addition to `POST …/suites` and `POST …/doc-pages`, which is now a **prerequisite** rather than a reminder; `tcms_write_case`'s create arm is **absent from the schema** until `ai_proposal` exists, because shipping a direct create first ships what the review surface exists to prevent and would then have to be taken away. **§8 question 2 falls out as a side effect** — the create/edit arm boundary *is* the new-vs-edit line. Also found: **`package-info.java` still says `doc_link` has no writer**, stale since §93 in the one file an agent reads first to orient, while `DocBlockView` records the supersession correctly — the four-documents-called-the-Linux-gate-inert shape, one module down. **(design only — no migration, no contract change, no code, and therefore no §65 rows; the spec's §8 specifies four computed checks the implementation owes, and the §6w reversals are not yet written back into §6w)** |

It is **a reversal of a recorded decision, not the filling of a hole**: both services *argued* against the header since §29 — *"a duplicate folder is visible in the tree the moment the create returns, next to its twin"* — and the argument was correct when written. **Its premise expired twice.** It reasons entirely from a person looking at a screen, and §135's tool surface adds a caller with no screen and therefore no correction loop; and **§98 deleted the tree the sentence names**, so half of it had been false for thirty-eight sections with nothing depending on it. Both javadocs are struck through in place. **The two endpoints are the cheapest and the most expensive duplicate of the six**: a duplicate suite holds nothing and deletes cleanly, while **a page is a LINK TARGET** — `doc_link` points at `(page_id, block_id)` and ERD resolved question #18 makes a `doc_block` this product's whole model of a requirement — so a duplicate page splits traceability across two sets of block ids and **deleting the loser destroys its links rather than merging them**, the one duplicate in the family a person cannot clean up by pressing delete. That the weakest copy of the argument sat where it mattered most is the cost of copying a reason instead of re-deriving it. **`V11`'s standing invitation to generalise is declined for the fourth time** at six columns over six tables, on `V17`'s argument, which does not weaken with the count: a general `idempotent_request` table must store the **rendered response**, and a stored response goes stale the moment the row it describes is edited — the column form re-renders through the same assembler the `GET` uses. **`V42` adds no table and no policy; the count stays 62.** The finding beside the feature is that **`MAX_IDEMPOTENCY_KEY = 200` is now declared in seven services across four modules and nothing had ever compared them** — and the shared constant is **refused**, because the only module all four may import is `tenancy` and an HTTP header's cap is not a tenancy concept (§125's bar, which refused `shared` as a grab bag). It becomes a **computed check** instead: `IdempotencyKeyCapTest`, no Spring context, which also asserts **the contract's `maxLength: 200` equals the code's cap** — the drift nobody could see, since a generated client trims to the contract's number and would produce a 400 its own types call impossible. Mutation `IK-6` is what makes that argument falsifiable rather than a preference. Smaller: the sealed `Outcome` broke both controllers' switches at compile time, which is why they enumerate success arms instead of defaulting; the shared `IdempotencyKey` parameter still said **"the case"** after six other creates adopted it; and **`documentation/package-info.java`'s "`doc_link` has no writer" is corrected** — §135 found it, stale since §93, in the file an agent reads first. **(built and unverified — seven mutation rows `IK-1` … `IK-7`, none run, two recorded as known gaps: nothing in this repo drives two concurrent creates carrying one key, on any of the six tables, and the doc-page scope test crosses companies rather than projects. No frontend row and no frontend change: the SPA still sends no key, because the old argument does hold for a person at a screen.)** |
| [§137](handoff/137-the-shared-ts-client-and-the-cli.md) | The shared TypeScript client and the CLI — the MCP plan's phases **4 and 5 together**, three phases, **no migration and no contract change**, and the first slice that adds a **stack** rather than a feature. `packages/` is a third npm root holding `@tcms/client` (typed over `openapi.yaml` through `openapi-fetch`, plus the surface spec's §4 command core) and `@tcms/cli` (nine read commands). **Phase 4 was not allowed to ship alone**: a generated client with no caller is the writerless shape §116/§117/§121/§132 each found, and §121's version was *deleted* rather than used. **Two silent defects, both in the transport, both found by a test written beside the code** — `openapi-fetch` calls `fetch(Request)` with no init, so the first draft sent every request as a bodiless GET; and a signal read back off a copy-constructed `Request` **never aborts**, measured, so a deadline handed to one is discarded and the request hangs — the failure the budget exists to prevent, arriving through the budget. **The finding beside the feature: surface spec §4.5 asks for something this credential cannot obtain** — naming the role that holds a missing permission needs `GET /roles`, gated on `company:read`, which `V40` withholds from `Agent` on purpose, so the refusal copy names the permission and not the role, which is strictly less than the spec permits and therefore the safe direction. **`schema.d.ts` has a twin now** and `schemaFreshness.test.ts` compares them byte-for-byte — the only thing anywhere that catches one stack regenerating after a contract change and the other not. `ci.yml` gained a third job, and unlike the backend job **it runs its tests**. **NO COMMAND HAS BEEN RUN AGAINST A REAL TCMS**;
