A subset check would have stayed green while a security-relevant justification quietly became false. Now `…MinusFive`. **Generalised: when a rule reads "X minus the things that…", assert the delta, never the subset.** One incoherence is **recorded and deliberately not fixed** — `LifecycleLadder` charges the *approve* key for `DEPRECATED` on the argument that retiring is the soft form of a Lead-only delete, so a Test Engineer may now **delete** a colleague's case and may not **deprecate** it; repairing it means moving a gate on the *approval ladder*, which was out of scope. `suite:delete`/`artifact:delete` (shared structure) and `run:close`/`result:amend` (judgements about the work) stay Lead-only, and author-cannot-approve-own-work is untouched — §A3.5's "creative vs destructive" is rewritten as "content vs shared structure and judgement", because the old framing collapsed *unmaking a colleague* with *unmaking shared structure*. `V12`, `V40` and `V3` still argue the old position and are **not edited** — an applied migration is immutable here, so `V51`'s header is where a grep for `V12` gets redirected, and the column comment is restated the way `V49` did it. **The frontend changed no behaviour and no frontend test failed**: every delete affordance gates on the key with `authorId` alongside, which an `ALL` qualifier makes a no-op, and the four gate tests **mock** the qualifier rather than reading the catalogue — so they still cover the retained mechanism. Three readings were live; the one taken (at the user's direction) is **decide it on the act's own terms** — and it turns out to be the rule `LifecycleLadder` already had for every other edge, so the implementation *shrank*. **The whole gate is now one sentence: the approve key buys a sign-off, and it buys touching an artifact that has one.** Five of the sixteen pairs move; §75's three — `APPROVED →` anything — are untouched. **`rank` went with it, and the deletion is the finding at the mechanism**: the ordinal put `DEPRECATED` at **3, above `APPROVED`**, and that single number is where both halves of the incoherence lived — ranking retirement above approval is what made retiring reachable only from the ladder's top *and* what made reviving read as an unmaking. Nothing ever argued it; the comment argues that the order is *"the domain's own — drafted before reviewed before approved before retired"*, which is a claim about **narrative order** being used as a claim about **authority**. `PlanLifecycle#rank` stays — `PlanStatus` has no `DEPRECATED`, so its three values really are a line. **The suite half was re-argued rather than split off**: `suite:delete` *removes* a folder while deprecating keeps it, keeps its members and can be undone, so they are not two strengths of one act — and `decide`'s `DEPRECATED`-from-anywhere rule has argued since M0 that the move must be **legal** from a draft to avoid a dead end, sitting for eighty slices beside a gate making it **unaffordable** from a draft. The dead end was closed on one layer and left open on the one above it. **Reviving is symmetric by decision, asked separately**, because fail-closed is the instinct and it makes retiring a **one-way door for exactly the caller the slice just let through it**; nothing is unmade coming back, since `standingOf` puts `DEPRECATED` `OUTSIDE_REVIEW` — the sign-off ended *at the retirement*, performed by somebody holding the approve key. **The find is that the header promised a test nobody wrote**: `LifecycleLadder` has said since §75 that *"a test can enumerate all sixteen pairs without a database"* and the gate's entire coverage was **five assertions across two endpoint tests**, each a container and a seeded persona, so the eleven pairs nobody named were invisible from inside either file — §205's shape, a description reading as evidence. `LifecycleLadderTest` is that table now, ~40 lines, total over the enum by construction, pinning the approve key's reach as seven strings in both directions. Also struck there: *"`case:delete` is the only `OWN`-qualified key"*, false since `V51`. `openapi.yaml`'s `BulkOperation` description was **corrected beyond this slice** — it still stated the target-decides rule §75 replaced and never mentioned the per-row half at all. | [§209](handoff/209-the-acts-that-did-not-exist.md) | **The acts that did not exist, and the three surfaces that advised them** — two phases, `backend/` + contract + both `schema` copies, then `frontend/`. No migration, no permission moved, no grant changed. §208's first opened item: `ArtifactStatus`'s two values had been in `V1`'s CHECK constraint and the enum since M0 with **no writer**, and the finding is not the enum — it is that **three shipped surfaces were already instructing users to perform the acts**, the delete's 409 (*"Supersede it with a correction instead"*), the upload form's helper text, and the contract's own delete description. `ui-rules` §4's mandated-follow-up rule failed three times over, each reading as documentation rather than as a promise, which is why none was ever checked. **The two acts are reached differently and the asymmetry is the model:** `ARCHIVED` is a judgement about this row, so it is chosen (`PUT …/artifacts/{id}/status`) and reversible; `SUPERSEDED` is a fact about **another** row — it holds exactly when `superseded_by_id` names a replacement — so it is neither chosen nor left, and `ArtifactStatusInput`'s enum is a **proper subset** of `Artifact.status`. Superseding is **one request**: `supersedes` on either create writes the replacement and flips the replaced row in one transaction, so there is no window where the newer document exists and the register calls the older one current. **The same-scope rule is the whole of the authorization** and it looks like tidiness: `supersedes` writes a second row nobody gated, and requiring it to sit at the same scope and in the same plan is what makes the create's own qualifier cover both — without it a Test Engineer registers a plan-scope note that retires the project's test strategy and gets a `201` that looks entirely correct. **A superseded row stays deletable, re-argued rather than inherited**: being replaced is not what makes a record permanent, `write_once` is, and refusing both ends would make a *mis-registered* link immortal the moment somebody corrected it. `superseded_by_id` is mapped — the **third** "blocked" note to outlive its blocker inside one entity, which is where it stops being a coincidence — and `ArtifactEndpointTest`'s raw-SQL `setSupersededBy` is deleted because the product can now produce the state. Three repository-wide rules caught the slice and **none was reached by a test written for it**: the schema pairing, the literal-sentence rule (which a parameterized `scopeProblem` handed a variable), and — on shanks, not locally — the §203 exchange harness plus `contractFixtures`. |

Test Engineer and up adds create, edit and delete; Test Lead adds giving approvals and adding and managing permission on the project; Company Admin anything in the company; Instance Admin anything in any company; And its callback carried a **caller-supplied copy of a fact the `state` already fixes** server-side, which on a pre-authentication surface is how a callback gets pointed at somebody else's provider. **The arrival state at phase 3 was RED**: phase 2 committed on a targeted `RepositoryTransactionTest` run, and an entity landing a phase ahead of its callers fails **four** `ArchitectureRulesTest` rules at once — reader, writer, repository caller, product-writable — none of them the class a persistence plan thinks to run. All four closed. **`ProviderSecrets` is the SECOND AES-GCM implementation** (`integration.SecretCipher` is the first) because `identity` may not depend on `integration` and a sixth foundation module is a decision rather than an import — a running tally, so **the third caller arrives with the extraction** — and its own asymmetry is written at both ends: `ApiTokenSecrets` and `InvitationTokens` **digest** because those are only checked, this one **encrypts** because a token exchange presents the plaintext. **Phase 7's finding is that the reference issuer cannot be registered through the product**: `validate` refuses a non-`https://` issuer (rightly — the `jwks_uri` every signature is checked against is fetched from it) and #37's compose Keycloak is plain HTTP. Phase 6 shipped the gate, Task 8 asked for the counterparty, **neither is wrong on its own and the plan never noticed they collide.** So the local provider row goes in by SQL as `postgres` (FORCE again, from the write side), the realm's client is **public** — which is what makes that possible, since `client_secret_encrypted` is ciphertext nothing outside the running application can produce — and whether `validate` grows a dev-only escape is left **open as a decision**, with plan 3's LDAP `allowInsecure` as the house precedent. Compose's three Keycloak settings are one argument: **one hostname, or the `iss` claim splits in two** — a browser on `localhost` and a backend on the compose network would be told two different issuers and `JwtIssuerValidator` would refuse the `id_token` after a sign-in that looked like it worked; a network alias plus `keycloak.localtest.me` resolving to 127.0.0.1 makes one URL work from both sides, verified from both. Keycloak in compose makes the flow *drivable* and buys no automated observer. **`SO-3` is expected to look broken and is not**: the browser ends at `/sso/callback#token=…` on an SPA with **no such route**, so a valid session sits in the address bar unread — that fragment is the specification for a `fe-react` lane **no plan covers**, and until it exists a person can complete an external sign-in and the product will not let them in. `FD-15` and `§218 NC-5` are the same missing rule two days apart: nothing here asserts a credential is absent from a *response* schema.)** |

A first for this repo; the pre-assignment convention is the entire mechanism. `V54` drops `team.notification_channel`, writerless since M0. `integration` is the thirteenth module, declares `{api, tenancy, project}` — **`identity` was named by the plan and left off**, because a declaration is a fact about code that exists and a display name arrives through `project.PeopleDirectory` — and it is **not** a foundation module, so `nothingDependsOnIntegration` pins the leaf claim from the other side. **Three things deliberately not built**: `work.defect_created` (no producer — `defect:create` is seeded and there is no defect entity anywhere, so the constant would be the writerless shape one level up from the column this migration drops), a project-level delivery log, and retry UI. | [§223](handoff/223-a-directory-is-a-second-kind-of-provider.md) | **A directory is a second kind of provider, and the whole difference is a POST** — nine phases + a docs phase, both stacks, `V57`, one new operation (`POST /auth/ldap/{providerId}/login`). a closed run **is** frozen, and that is a judgement with its own sentence rather than `CLOSED_MESSAGE`'s. **`GET /projects/{projectId}/configurations` came with it and is not in the audit's text** — the environment list lived only on `TestPlanDetail`, so a run scoped by suites or tags (§99) had nowhere to read it from, which is the whole of `RunCloneDialog`'s recorded *"there is no configuration picker anywhere in this SPA"*; the audit's B1 is deliberately not pre-empted. `write_once` is refused with 409, **and a `SUPERSEDED` row is editable** — `setStatus` refuses it because a *status* is a fact about another row and a title is not — which forced the UI change worth knowing: **the row kebab no longer shuts on a superseded row**, or the register's one edit path would be out of reach on every row anybody had ever replaced. `DELETE` shipped in §126 with both 409 guards and had **no caller at all**; materialisation-once is untouched and the flag is refused by name when there is nothing recorded. | [§234](handoff/234-the-register-that-could-finally-hold-a-report.md) | **The register that could finally hold a report, and the promise it could not make** — **C6 + C7 + C10**, slice 2 of the C run (after §233), in the audit's §3.6 order. **No migration, no permission added or moved**: every column this slice writes has been in `V1` since M0, which is the shape of the whole thing — `artifact` had two writerless columns and the register had no agent surface, and closing them together is why the MCP tools could arrive complete rather than as a delete with no read. **C7 — `write_once` was enforced for three slices and settable by nobody**: the delete honoured it from §126 and the edit from §229, and both tests reached the state with `UPDATE artifact SET write_once = true`. Registration sets it now and both fixtures are deleted. Three properties are the design: **`updatable = false`** (a promise that can be made later can be made about somebody else's row; one that can be taken back is not a promise), **it is on the wire** — the §31 half, because both refusals were `409`s the client could not predict, so Correct… and Remove… enabled on every sign-off row and the register answered after the correction was typed — and **archive and supersede stay allowed**, both asserted, because circulation is not content and a write-once row nobody could supersede would be a record nobody could correct. **C6 — the request names the JOB and the row keeps the ARTIFACT.** `report_artifact` has no `project_id` (`V1` scoped it by company and definition), so an artifact id cannot be gated against the path's project and a job id can; what is stored is the artifact because that row is immutable, so a pinned entry answers the same bytes for ever and regenerating leaves it alone. `ReportFacts` is `PlanFacts`' pattern, fifth instance in the codebase. `ArtifactView.url` has three arms now and the third is gated as a **report** (`report:export`) — pinning records that it was produced, it does not re-license carrying it off. On the SPA "Save to register" **reuses `RegisterArtifactDialog`** rather than carrying a second form, which is that file's own recorded rule: pinning differs in one respect, *what the payload is*, and that is the one thing the user never chooses. **C10 — `tcms_list_artifacts` + `tcms_write_artifact` (register/edit/status/delete, delete behind §210's consent prompt), 21 tools → 23.** §211's "no artifact tools by decision" note is struck through in place: its condition — a delete needs a read that serves the ids — was **satisfied, not waived**. `FILE` still cannot be registered there (a tool call carries no bytes, `tcms_record_result`'s wall), so `register` derives its kind from the payload named rather than taking a `kind` that could disagree. **Two findings the plan did not contain, both from checks.** `PATCH …/artifacts/{artifactId}` declares no `If-Match` and cannot simply gain one — `Artifact` carries no `lockVersion` on the wire — so it is recorded as a decision with its cost. And **the MCP capability summary silently lost its "ask for this permission" section**: two tools took it to 1848 bytes, past the host's 2KB ceiling, and `fit` dropped it whole — it was always the section destined to be sacrificed as the registry grew, for the credential that needs it most, and a dropped section reads exactly like a credential with nothing hidden. It degrades to the keys alone now, with a test. Gates on shanks: backend **BUILD SUCCESSFUL** (7m59s), frontend **1508 / 111**, packages **336** (78/130/128). Queue: `§234 AR-1…AR-4` and `§234 PM-1…PM-2`, none run. Not done: **C3 (next), C5+C9, C2, C1, C4** — five of eleven C items remain. |
| [§235](handoff/235-the-vector-that-had-no-reader.md) | **The vector that had no reader** — **C3**, slice 3 of the C run, in the audit's §3.6 order. Two phases: contract + backend, then the search box. **No migration, no permission added or moved, no new key** — every column read here has been in `V1` since M0 and every trigger filling it in `V2` just as long, which is the shape of the whole thing: **four tables carry a `search_vector`, all four GIN-indexed, all four trigger-maintained, and nothing queried any of them** — while `PlanDocumentPanel` justified deleting its own editor on pages being *"found by everybody who searches documentation"*, and told its empty state to write the page elsewhere so it would keep *"its place in search"*. **The decision of the slice is the tenancy one, and it is a misreading corrected rather than a trade accepted.** `CaseSpecifications#keyOrText` declines this operator partly because a native query "drops the Hibernate company filter and leaves RLS as the only boundary, the single-point-of-defence trade `ProjectRepository`'s header refuses" — **and that header refuses no such thing**: it writes a native query and spells `company_id = :companyId` out beside RLS, because "the predicate is the intent and the two agreeing is what makes a mismatch return nothing rather than something", and `DocBlockRepository` says the same at two sites in this very module. So `DocPageRepository#search` is native with **three** application predicates over RLS — company from `CompanyContext`, never from request input — which is more boundary than the filtered Criteria query the case list uses, not less; a `FunctionContributor` was considered and rejected as a dialect-wide seam for one fixed-shape query. All three stale comments are struck through in place, because the misreading is what would send a fourth author around the vector. **The other half of that refusal survives and is measured rather than asserted**: matching is by lexeme, the stemmer runs on both sides (so `refundable` finds "refunds"), and what fails is the **prefix** — `refun` finds nothing. Disqualifying for a typeahead, correct for prose read on submit, which is why the condition and shared-step libraries stayed on `LIKE` and why **the SPA's box submits rather than filtering per keystroke** (also sidestepping §186's debounce-and-detached-node trap). **Only ids come back from the native query**: the entities load through a filtered JPQL read and are assembled by the same `DocPageSummaryView.of` the tree renders, so a result row and the sidebar row beside it cannot disagree about a title, and a page the ranking named but the entity read did not return is dropped rather than served empty. `ts_headline` runs over the same text the vector was built from, so the fragment always contains the match; **plain text with no `<mark>`** (emitting markup would put escaping of stored content on the server) and **null for a blockless page**, served rather than omitted. `q` lives in the URL, so reload, deep link and Back all survive, and `suffix` carries it onto every link so opening a hit keeps the results. **One inherited finding recorded rather than fixed**: `V2` extracts *every* string in a block's JSON, so `blockType` is in the corpus and searching `table` matches every page containing one — a trigger change and a migration, not this slice's. **One red that pre-dated the slice and is closed by it**: `ReportViewPage.test.tsx` had been failing on master since §234, whose "Save to register" control added a permission read and a plan read that both fell through the double's catch-all arm and were answered with a *project* — verified pre-existing by stashing §235 entirely and re-running at phase 1. The reusable shape: **a fall-through arm in a test double silently adopts every read its page gains afterwards.** Gates on shanks: backend **3026 / 0 / 0** (+10), `packages` **336**, frontend **1529 / 113 files** (+7 new, +7 recovered); the SQL was additionally probed against a throwaway Postgres 17 first, and two of its three constructs did not behave as first written. Queue: `§235 DS-1…DS-6` (lane A1) and `§235 PM-1…PM-3`, none run. **Six of eleven C items done**; remaining, in order: **C5 + C9, C2, C1, C4**. |
| [§236](handoff/236-the-defect-link-and-the-steps-evidence.md) | **The defect link and the step's evidence** — **C5 + C9**, slice 4 of the C run, in the audit's §3.6 order. One migration (`V62`), **no permission seeded, none moved**. **C5 gave `defect_link` its first writer in the product's history**: the table has been in `V1` since M0, is read by three screens, and `defect:create` was seeded by `V3` in the same milestone with no consumer. **Four decisions the schema cannot answer, and none of them defaults safely.** (1) **The link hangs off the EXECUTION.** Both FKs are nullable, so this was a real choice; every reader is execution-scoped (`findDefectLinks` is `WHERE d.execution_id IN (…)`) and **nothing reads `case_id`**, which therefore stays NULL rather than being filled on the reasoning that a later query might want it. A case-scoped link is a different product — *this defect concerns this case in general* rather than *this run of it* — with no screen and no permission argument. (2) **Entity for the write, native for the read** — and the read's javadoc claiming it was native *because there is no writer* is struck through in place (the §152 shape, **fourth slice in a row**); the surviving reason is that it is a batch projection over N executions and the mapping deliberately **omits `status_cache`**, a column the read serves. `findDefectLinks` now spells `company_id` out beside RLS (§235's rule), which is also what makes the new index's leading column usable. (3) **`provider` loses its `DEFAULT 'JIRA'` — and gains no input field either**, which is the half worth reading: dropping the default stops the *database* naming a product the instance has never spoken to, but a **caller-supplied** `JIRA` would be worse, because it reads as somebody's deliberate statement rather than as a column nobody filled in, and pre-M4 there is nothing to check it against. The server writes `EXTERNAL` — *a link a person made* — the only thing this writer can say truthfully. (4) **`status_cache` and `synced_at` stay writerless deliberately, and this is NOT §234's C7 pointing the other way.** The difference is what NULL says: `status_cache` is declared nullable and documented *"as of the last sync"*, so a never-synced row has NULL **truthfully**, where `DEFAULT 'JIRA'` asserted a fact nobody established. So they keep their columns, gain no writer, and **no caller may set them** — a client-supplied status is one person's guess recorded as a tracker's answer, the exact claim `V62` removes. **Two things the schema was lying about, found on the way**: `DefectLink.url` has been **required on the wire and nullable in the column** since M0 — unviolated only because the table had no writer, which is also why `SET NOT NULL` is provably safe here rather than merely probably — and the table had **no index at all beyond its primary key**, so the read was a sequential scan waiting to happen; one partial unique index on `(company_id, execution_id, provider, external_key)` now serves the read (its prefix *is* the lookup) **and enforces the 200-on-replay**, so two concurrent retries of one link collide on the constraint instead of both writing. **The `DELETE` is charged `defect:create`**: there is no `defect:delete`, the catalogue has seeded no key since `V3`, and the alternative — a create with no correction path — leaves a mistyped key rendering as a broken `href` on three screens forever; stated at the site, with **no authorship gate**, because nothing has carried a qualifier since `V51` and inventing a row-scoped rule the catalogue does not express would be the service granting itself something. A closed run freezes both writes, mirroring `EvidenceService`. **`javascript:` is refused at the write** — three screens put this value in an `href` and React does not sanitise one; re-stated from `documentation.ExternalLinkService` rather than shared, because `execution` may not depend on `documentation`. **C9 is frontend-only exactly as the audit said, and this slice's first draft doubted it and was wrong** — it proposed adding `Evidence.stepId` on the belief that step-attached files landed indistinguishably in the flat list. They never did: `StepResult.evidence` has been **required and served** the whole time, `EvidenceViews.partition` splits the rows server-side and `ExecutionViews.steps` hangs each list off its step. The gap was one component, and it read as "handled" because `ExecutionPage.tsx` mentioned `evidence` exactly once, at the `EvidencePanel` mount. `StepEvidence` renders each step's files and passes `stepId`, **gated on the server's `statusId` and never on the local mark** — the runner holds verdicts in state until Submit while the endpoint 400s on a step with no `step_outcome`, so an affordance keyed on the click would have failed every time; the disabled tooltip says the server's own sentence. **One stale claim corrected that this slice did not cause** (`Evidence.createdBy`'s contract description still taught `evidence:delete`'s `OWN` qualifier, emptied by `V51`, while the delete operation forty lines away already said so) **and three more recorded and NOT fixed** — `Evidence.java` (including a whole `<h2>` claiming the run-closed invariant is unenforced, which §72 enforced) and `EvidenceService` twice. They survive because `tierClaims.test.ts` has no *"is `OWN`-qualified for"* shape: **the instrument gap is the finding, not the three sites.** Gates on shanks: backend **3038 / 0 / 0** (+12), frontend **1547 / 115** (+18 on a 1529 baseline: 14 new `it` blocks plus four per-file sweep entries), packages **336**, both `schema` copies regenerated and identical. Arrival was gated first: 1529/113 frontend, backend `UP-TO-DATE` against §235's 3026 — both `CLAUDE.md` counts were stale and are corrected here. Queue: `§236 DK-1…DK-7` + `SE-1…SE-5` (browser, lane `A3`) and `§236 DK-1…DK-7` + `SE-1` (mutation), none run; **two mutation rows are expected GREEN and recorded as gaps** — nothing drives a link against a closed run, and nothing asserts the step-evidence refusal. **Eight of eleven C items done**; remaining, in order: **C2, C1, C4**. |
| [§237](handoff/237-the-flag-nothing-read.md) | **The flag nothing read, and the pass rate that was allowed to move** — **C2**, slice 5 of the C run, in the audit's §3.6 order. `case_flake.quarantined` has been written by `CaseQuarantineService` since `V38` and read by **nothing downstream**; this slice gave it two consumers. **No migration, no permission seeded or moved**, one contract property. The audit's C2 row is one sentence and contains **three decisions it does not make**, all of which went against the obvious reading. (1) **LIVE, not snapshotted onto `execution_fact` — and the architecturally tidy answer is the inert one.** A frozen `quarantined` dimension would sit comfortably beside `automation_status` and `suite_path`, and it would do nothing: a test is quarantined *because* it went red, **within the hour** (`CaseFlake.unscored` says so in those words), so every result a reader wants discounted was filed *before* the decision and a frozen column reads `false` on exactly those rows — inert on the day it is used, working only on results filed later, which is a build that works rendering identically to one that does not (§47/§53/§72/§81). So the flag is read at report time and the cost is **stated rather than hidden**: quarantining today moves the excluded figure for last month. Two consequences: `execution_fact` gains no column, and `recomputeFlakeScores`' standing warning that an `EXCLUDED.*` set-list "would release every quarantined test on the next tick" is **still true and about a statement nothing here edits** — re-read at the end to confirm. (2) **BOTH RATES PRINTED, and the headline is the one that does not move.** The audit says *exclude from the denominator*; taken literally that replaces the printed figure, and it is refused twice. §6e refuses it on its own terms — a lone 95.2% cannot be told from a 95.2% with nothing quarantined — and `CaseQuarantineService`'s header wrote this slice's specification two milestones in advance: *"when gating reads this column it must say so on the face of the report — a separate slice, with §6e's discipline."* **And `AggregateExecutionCrossCheckTest` refuses it structurally**: it pins this report's per-status counts against `GET /plans/{planId}/statistics`, and `PlanStatisticsService` lives in `execution`, a module that **may not read `case_flake` at all** — so an exclusion applied here and not there would break the guard §46 paid for *by design*, leaving nothing to tell it from the drift it exists to catch. The shape is §105's blocked-vs-failed one milestone on: the original rate unchanged, a second `Pass rate (excluding quarantined cases)` beside it, a `Quarantined cases in this selection` count **printed at zero**, and a section naming the case, the **reason somebody typed**, and when. **The exclusion is one figure wide and `nothingElseInTheReportMoves` asserts it** — status breakdown, per-tester, per-suite, feature, automation and retry sections all count the quarantined rows in full, or the sections stop summing to the headline in the direction nobody checks (§96). The section lists every quarantine in the **project** while the metadata counts the **contributing subset** — different numbers on purpose, reconciled in the narrative, because a quarantined case with nothing in the selection is a test *nobody is running at all*. **Widened to `RunSummaryReport`** at the user's direction (a run's pass rate is what a release decision is taken on); `PlanStatisticsService` could not follow, so the run page's Result bar and the run report's headline still agree and only the report carries the second figure — named here so it is not read later as an oversight. (3) **THE BADGE IS ON THE CHECKLIST, not only on the screen it opens.** `TestExecution` is an `allOf` over `TestExecutionSummary`, so one property serves both and the real question was whether the checklist was worth the crossing: a badge you see only after opening the execution is one you see **after you have already chosen the row**. It renders in all three places — Grid checklist, Focus queue rail, execution header — and the rail is the one that had to earn its 260px, because that is where the next case is *picked*. Cost: one query per run. `ExecutionViews`' header said **"Four bulk lookups, never per row"** and says **five** now, in the same commit; `CaseFacts` gained a twelfth crossing, `quarantinedCaseIds`, keyed on **case id and never execution id** (two uuids of the same shape on one line). **It never means skip it**: the row stays enabled, takes a verdict with no confirmation, and still counts towards the run's own pass rate; the chip is outlined amber beside the *title* rather than filled in the *Result* column, and the tooltip says in words that the case is still to be run — "Quarantined" alone reads as "don't bother", the one thing it must not mean. The chip deliberately cannot say **why** (the reason is a sentence, the checklist is a table; `FlakePanel` and the board card render it in full). **The crossing goes through `ExecutionSource` and not into the report**, because `reporting/package-info.java` permits exactly one class to name another module's tables and warns it would be ended "for the most tempting reason there is — that the new query is short"; that header now says **eight** tables, not seven. The empty-set predicate is the literal `false` (`IN ()` is not valid SQL) and the `FILTER`s sit on **`resolved`, not `selected`**, so the exclusion follows the aggregation policy — 2 under `ALL`, 1 under the default, asserted. **One deliberate divergence from `work.BoardFacts.quarantinedCases`**: that drops soft-deleted cases and this does not, because a board card is work to do and a report figure is history to account for, and the fact log projects a deleted case's results anyway. **One stale claim corrected that this slice did not cause**: `RunSummaryReport`'s header said the per-case list *"waits for the slice that decides where case labels come from"* — `ExecutionSource.caseLabels` landed in **R7** and its own javadoc names that header as what it was answering, so the two files have contradicted each other for eight rounds. **Fifth slice in a row to hit the §152 shape**, and the pattern is now stateable: *a comment describing an absence is never revisited by the slice that ends the absence*, because that slice is working in a different file. **`npm run build` earned its keep again** (§236's lesson, second outing): five test files build a `TestExecutionSummary` by hand instead of calling the fixture, so a required property broke `tsc -b` in five places where every one of those tests would still have run — plus a MUI v7 `Stack` refusing a top-level `alignItems`. Not done and deliberately: `PlanStatisticsService` and the coverage matrix carry no second figure; **the MCP surface needed nothing** (its run reads return the response whole, so `quarantined` already reaches an agent, and no tool description enumerates fields); no new permission, catalogue stays 55. Gates on shanks: backend **BUILD SUCCESSFUL** (+16 tests), frontend build + lint + suite, packages **336** unchanged; arrival baseline run first at **1547 / 115**, matching §236. Queue: `§237 QB-1…QB-6` + `QR-1…QR-7` (browser, lane `A3` — **`QR-1` must be read *before* anything is quarantined**, since the finding is the difference between two readings of one report) and `§237 QR-1…QR-7` + `QB-1…QB-3` (mutation), none run; **`QR-7` is expected green and recorded as a gap**, not a decision. **Nine of eleven C items done**; remaining, in order: **C1, C4**. |
